within myself, Gentle Reader,
how many counterfeit books
and false recipes of the

composed by the fraud and greed of imposters,
in which not even a spark of truth shines forth,

deceived, and are still being deceived—I judged
that I could do nothing more useful than

-># PREFACE.<-

support and confirmation of the Alchemical truth <note>original: "veritatis Chymicæ"</note> which has been undeservedly proclaimed, I neither should nor could hide it or shroud it in silence; even though in these malicious times (where virtue and vice are held as equals), because of the unworthiness of the age and the ingratitude and faithlessness of men—to say nothing of the curses of the Philosophers <note>Alchemical authors traditionally "cursed" anyone who would reveal their secrets to the common or unworthy person.</note>—it is greatly afraid to come out into the public. I could bring forward witnesses of this Alchemical truth, namely most serious authors, according to the unanimous consensus of venerable Antiquity across so many different Nations: But, those things which are seen by ocular experience <note>original: "experimento oculari," meaning direct observation or witnessing with one's own eyes.</note> require no proof. In these past years (I speak from knowledge), many men of both the highest and lowest rank have seen that **DIANA** without her veil <note>original: "sine veste Dianam." In alchemical literature, Diana (the moon goddess) represents silver or the feminine principle of nature; to see her "without a veil" or "without clothing" is a metaphor for perceiving the naked truth of Nature's secrets without obfuscation.</note>: and although some idle troublemakers <term>maleferiati</term> <note>Latin for "those who keep holiday at the wrong time," used to describe people who are idle, misguided, or engaged in useless or harmful activities.</note> are found, who—whether out of envy, malice, or fear of their impostures being detected—cry out that the soul of gold can be extracted, and through a pompous and vain delusion of ostentation, be returned to another body, not without the loss of time, labor, and expense—

<sig>A 3</sig>

<summary>The author argues that alchemical truth is self-evident to those who have witnessed it through experience, comparing the revelation of nature's secrets to seeing the goddess Diana without her veil. He warns against "idle troublemakers" and impostors who claim to extract the "soul of gold" only to deceive others.</summary>
<keywords>Alchemical truth, Antiquity, Diana, observation, imposture, soul of gold, maleferiati</keywords>

### PREFACE.

loss and detriment: let the *sons* of Hermes <note>original: "Hermetis filij"; a traditional term for students and practitioners of alchemy who follow the legendary teachings of Hermes Trismegistus</note> know for certain that the extraction of such "souls," as they call them, whether from *Gold* or from *the Moon* <note>the alchemical name for Silver</note> (by whatever common *Alchemical* way), is a mere delusion. Indeed, many do not believe this, but at length it is proven true through experience—the one and only mistress of truth—though only after great loss. On the contrary, he who (by the *Philosophical* way) can perform this without fraud or deception—so that he truly dyes <term>tingat: to "tincture" or permanently change the color and nature of a metal</term> even the smallest amount of metal, whether for profit or not, with the permanent color of the *Sun* <note>Gold</note> or *the Moon* (remaining so through all required tests)—of that man I can rightly affirm that he has the doors of *Nature* open to him, allowing him to investigate further and higher secrets, and to acquire them with the blessing of **GOD**. Furthermore, I offer these present Treatises, written from my own experience, to the sons of science, so that while they occupy all their thoughts and mental energy in inquiring into the hidden operations of *Nature*, they may perceive the truth of things and *Nature* herself; and that they may come to know in which single *Matter* <note>original: "Re"; referring to the "Prima Materia" or the starting substance of the alchemical work</note> the whole of this most holy *Philosophical Art*—

<summary>The author warns that "extracting souls" from metals via common alchemy is a delusion, insisting that true mastery is demonstrated by permanent transmutation. He offers his writings to true seekers to help them discover the singular matter required for the alchemical "Art."</summary>

<keywords>sons of Hermes, transmutation, gold and silver, philosophical way, mistress of truth, secrets of nature, alchemical treatises, Prima Materia</keywords>

# PREFACE.

perfection consists, provided they proceed by the **Royal Road** <note>original: "via Regia"; a common alchemical metaphor for the direct and true path of the Great Work</note>, which **Nature** prescribes in all operations. Therefore, I would here wish to warn the sincere reader to understand my writings not so much from the "bark" <note>the literal or surface level of the text</note> of the words, but from the powers of **Nature**, lest he later deplore the time, labor, and expense spent in vain. Let him consider that this is the science of the **Sages** <note>original: "Sophorum"; referring to the wise masters of alchemy</note>, not of the unskilled: and that the meaning of the Philosophers is far different than can be perceived by boastful braggarts <note>original: "Thrasones"; a reference to a stock character of a swaggering soldier in Roman comedy</note>, or learned mockers, or those who are vicious against their own conscience (who, while they cannot rise by virtues, strive to do so through crimes and slanders against honest men), or unskilled quacks <note>original: "circumforanei"; literally "market-wanderers" or traveling mountebanks</note>, who with their "whitenings" <term>dealbationibus: the alchemical process of *albedo*, turning the matter white</term> and "reddenings" <term>rubefactionibus: the process of *rubedo*, the final stage of turning the matter red</term> have deceived nearly the whole world, to the most unworthy defamation of the most praised **Chemical** science. For it is the gift of **God**, since it can only be reached by the grace of **God** alone illuminating the intellect, through patient and religious humility, or through the ocular demonstration of an experienced teacher—

<sig>A 4</sig>

<summary>The author warns that true alchemy follows the "Royal Road" of Nature and is a divine gift reserved for the humble and wise, rather than boastful quacks or mockers. He cautions readers to look beyond the literal surface of his words to understand the true forces of Nature.</summary>

# PREFACE.

stration. Wherefore, those who are estranged from God, He deservedly drives far away from His secrets. As for what remains; I uniquely ask the **Sons of the Art** <note>The "Sons of the Art" (*filii artis*) is a traditional term for true students and practitioners of alchemy who are initiated into its mysteries through study and divine grace.</note> that they receive with a grateful heart my zeal for deserving well of them; and when they have made the **HIDDEN MANIFEST** <note>original: "OCCULTUM ... MANIFESTUM". A central alchemical maxim referring to the process of bringing the internal, spiritual essence of a substance into a visible, physical form.</note>, and by the will of God, through constant labor, have arrived at the longed-for **Philosophical port** <note>original: "portum Philosophicum". A navigational metaphor representing the successful completion of the Great Work (*Magnum Opus*) and the attainment of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, let them, in the Philosophical manner, exclude all unworthy persons from this art. Mindful of love toward their needy neighbor in the fear of God, and having set aside vain ostentation, let them sing eternal praises of gratitude to the **Best and Thrice-Greatest** <note>original: "optimo ter Maximo". While a standard epithet for God (Jupiter/Jehovah), in an alchemical context it also alludes to Hermes Trismegistus, the "Thrice-Greatest" legendary founder of the hermetic arts.</note> for this special gift, without abuse, within a quiet and piously rejoicing heart.

->*of truth.*<-

### FIRST TREATISE.

# ON NATURE
## WHAT IT IS,
## *and what kind of seekers of it*
## *there should be.*

**M**any wise and most learned men, many centuries ago—indeed, as **Hermes** <note>original: "Hermete". Refers to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure regarded as the founder of alchemy and the "Hermetic" arts.</note> testifies, even before the flood—wrote much about the making of the **Philosopher's Stone** <note>original: "lapide Philosophorum". The legendary alchemical substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting longevity.</note>, and they left us so many writings that, unless Nature performed credible things for us daily, hardly anyone would now believe Nature to be real, because in previous ages there were not so many **inventors** <note>original: "inuentores". Here meaning those who discover or uncover the hidden mechanisms of the natural world.</note> of things. Nor did our predecessors consider anything else besides Nature itself and the **possibility of Nature** <note>original: "Naturæ possibilitatem". A technical term in alchemy referring to the inherent potential within natural matter to be transformed or perfected by the artist.</note>. And although they remained only in the simple way of Nature, they nevertheless found those things which we now, in so many re-

<summary>This page introduces the first treatise on the nature of alchemy, arguing that ancient masters like Hermes Trismegistus succeeded by following the simple path of Nature rather than complex inventions.</summary>
<keywords>Nature, Hermes Trismegistus, Philosopher's Stone, alchemical history, natural potential, ancient wisdom</keywords>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion from the previous page regarding the limitations of modern intellect compared to the simple path of Nature followed by the ancient sages. The sentence begins mid-word, completing the thought that modern people, "engaged in [various] affairs," could scarcely have imagined what the ancients discovered.</meta>

# ON MERCURY.

...engaged in so many affairs, could scarcely have imagined with our own wits. This happens because Nature and the generation of things in the world now seem common and simple to us. Because of this, we direct our intellects not toward things already known, but toward those which cannot happen, or can barely be achieved. This is why we succeed in this way: it is easier for us to devise subtle things—imagining things which even the Philosophers <note>original: "Philosophi"; in this context, the author refers to the ancient alchemical masters and natural sages</note> themselves did not know—than it is to reach the true course of Nature and the intended meaning of those Philosophers.

Human nature is so composed that it neglects what it already knows and always seeks other things; this is even more true of human intellects, to which Nature is subject. For example, you see any craftsman <term>craftsman (artifex): a skilled practitioner or artist; in alchemical literature, this often refers to the alchemist as the "artifex" who assists Nature's work</term> who, having reached the highest perfection of his craft, either seeks new things, misuses his skill, or simply stops. In the same way, noble Nature always acts, continuing until the very *Iliadum* <note>original: "Iliadum"; a specialized term in Paracelsian philosophy referring to the final stage of a thing's development or the "greatest cycle" of a process</note>, that is, the ultimate limit <term>ultimate limit (Terminum vltimum): the final point of a natural process or the end of a life cycle</term>, and afterward it ceases. For a certain permission was granted to Nature from the beginning, so that through its entire course it might arrive at better things and find full rest, toward which with every effort it ten- <note>The text ends mid-word with the catchword "dit," which would complete the word "tendit" (tends) on the next page.</note>

<summary>The author argues that modern thinkers struggle to understand Nature because they overlook its simplicity in favor of complex, impossible fantasies. He compares Nature's drive toward completion (the "Iliadum") to a craftsman who works until his masterpiece is finished.</summary>

<keywords>Nature, human intellect, Iliadum, ultimate limit, craftsman, alchemical philosophy, perfection, natural cycle</keywords>

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion from the previous page regarding the natural progression of things toward their completion. The text opens mid-sentence, concluding a thought about how Nature proceeds toward its final limit or "Iliadum."</meta>

# ON MERCURY.

...proceeds, and rejoices in its own end, not otherwise than the Ant in its old age, for whom Nature creates wings in its final years <note>original: "Formica senio"; a reference to the folk belief that ants growing wings was a sign of impending death or the final stage of their life</note>. In the same way, truly, our own wits have progressed so far—especially in the <term>Philosophical Art (Arte Philosophica): the traditional term for alchemy, specifically the pursuit of the Philosopher’s Stone</term>, or the practice of the Stone—that we have now almost reached the *Iliadum* <note>original: "Iliadum"; a Paracelsian term referring to the ultimate completion or the highest state of a thing's development</note> itself. For the <term>Art of Chemistry (Ars Chymiæ): the experimental and technical branch of alchemy focused on laboratory procedures</term> now discovers such subtleties that hardly any greater could be found, and it differs from the Art of the ancient Philosophers as much as a Clockmaker differs from a simple Blacksmith. And although both work with Iron, neither would understand the labor of the other, even though both are Masters in their own craft. 

If Hermes himself, the father of Philosophers <note>original: "Hermes"; referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of the hermetic and alchemical tradition</note>, and **GEBER** of subtle wit <note>original: "Geber"; the Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan, an 8th-century pioneer of chemistry</note>, along with the most profound **RAYMOND LULL** <note>original: "RAYMVNDO LVLLIO"; a 13th-century polymath and mystic associated with many alchemical works</note> were to return to life today, they would not be considered Philosophers by our modern Chemists, but rather mere students. They would be ignorant of so many distillations <term>distillation (distillatio): the process of vaporizing and condensing a liquid to purify it</term> used today, so many circulations <term>circulation (circulatio): a process where a liquid is distilled and returned to its residue repeatedly in a closed vessel</term>, so many calcinations <term>calcination (calcinatio): the process of heating a substance to a high temperature to reduce it to a powder or "calx"</term>, and so many other countless works of craftsmen which men of this century have discovered and devised from their writings. Only one thing is missing for us to know, that is...

<sig>A 6</sig>

<summary>This page discusses the immense technical progress of modern chemistry compared to the ancient alchemical masters, using the analogy of a clockmaker versus a blacksmith. The author argues that while moderns have perfected laboratory techniques like distillation and calcination, they have reached a state of extreme complexity compared to the simplicity of the ancients.</summary>

### CONCERNING MERCURY.

...which they themselves made, namely the <term>Philosopher's Stone<note>The legendary substance believed to transform base metals into gold and provide the elixir of life.</note></term> or the <term>Physical Tincture<note>original: "Tincturam Physicam"; another name for the Philosopher's Stone, emphasizing its ability to "tinge" or permeate and transform the inner nature of matter.</note></term>. While we seek it, we find other things; and if human procreation were not so common, and if Nature did not hold her law in that matter, we would now surely wander astray.

But to return to my purpose: I promised in this first treatise to explain Nature, so that vain imagination does not deflect us from the simple, true path. I say, therefore, that **NATURE** is one, true, simple, and whole in her being, which God made before the ages, and within her he enclosed a spirit. Know, however, that the limit and end of Nature is God, who is also the beginning of Nature; for no beginning is finished except in that in which it begins. 

I have said she is the only one through whom God does all things—not that God would not know how to work without her (since he himself made Nature and is omnipotent)—but because it pleased him so, and he did it. All things come from that one Nature, nor is there anything in the world outside of Nature. And although <term>abortions<note>original: "abortus"; in an alchemical context, this refers to failed results, malformations, or "monsters" produced when the process goes wrong.</note></term> sometimes occur, this is not a fault of Nature, but a defect of either the <term>craftsman<note>original: "artificis"; the alchemist or operator conducting the work.</note></term> or the place. This Nature is divided into [four] places...

### CONCERNING MERCURY.

...four, in which she performs all these works that are visible and yet exist in the shadow; for indeed, things exist in shadow rather than truly appearing. She is transformed into male and female, and is likened to <term>Mercury<note>In alchemy, Mercury (Mercurius) is the universal mediating principle—the "spirit" that connects the soul to the body and allows for the transformation of matter.</note></term> because she joins herself to various places; according to the locations of the earth—whether good or bad—she produces things, even though there are no truly "bad" places on Earth, as it might seem to us.

However, there are only **four qualities**, and these are present in all things, yet they never agree; one always exceeds another. **NATURE** herself is not visible, although she acts visibly, for she is a <term>volatile spirit<note>original: "spiritus volatilis"; a subtle, moving force or substance that cannot be easily contained or seen, acting as the "life force" within physical bodies.</note></term> who performs her duty within bodies. She has her place and seat in the **Divine Will**. 

For our purposes in this passage, she is useful to us for nothing else except that we might know her "places"—those which are more suitable or nearer to her. That is to say, we must know how to join thing to thing according to Nature, lest wood be mixed with man, or an ox or animal with metal; instead, let everything act upon its own kind, and then Nature will perform her duty. The "place" of Nature is no other than what was stated above: in the **Will of God**.

<summary>This page defines Nature as an invisible, volatile spirit acting under Divine Will through the four qualities. It emphasizes the alchemical principle of "like acting upon like," instructing the practitioner to follow natural affinities rather than mixing incompatible substances.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion regarding the operation of Nature. ### CONCERNING MERCURY.

<meta>catchword: TRA-</meta>

**Searchers of Nature** [Scrutatores Naturae] ought to be such as **Nature** herself is: truthful, simple, patient, and constant; and what is most important, they must be pious, fearing God, and doing no harm to their neighbor. Afterward, they should diligently consider whether their purpose accords with Nature, or if it is even possible. Let them learn from visible examples: specifically, from what things a thing is made, in what way, and in what kind of vessel Nature operates. 

For if you simply wish to do something as Nature itself does, then follow Nature. But if you truly strive for something more excellent than Nature—to improve upon her work—observe in what and through what a thing is improved, and it will always be done within its own **kind** <note>original: "simili"; refers to the Hermetic principle that "like produces like." One cannot create a substance using unrelated materials.</note>. 

For example, if you desire to advance a **metal** (which is our intention) further in <term>virtue<note>original: "virtute"; here meaning the inherent power, potency, or essential quality of a substance.</note></term> than Nature has made it, it is necessary to take the **Metallic Nature**, and this in <term>male and female<note>original: "mare & foemina"; in alchemical theory, these are the two complementary principles—usually Sulfur (the active/male) and Mercury (the passive/female)—which must be combined to generate the "offspring" or the perfected metal.</note></term>; otherwise, you will achieve nothing. For if you intend to create **metal** from herbs, you will labor in vain, just as you will not produce wood from a dog or a beast.

<summary>This page outlines the moral character required of an alchemist and establishes the core principle of "like produces like," arguing that metallic transformation requires starting with metallic "seeds" or principles.</summary>

### SECOND TREATISE.
# ON THE OPERATION
## OF NATURE IN OUR
->*purpose and seed.*<-

I have already said that Nature is true, unique, appearing everywhere, and continuous; she is recognized by the things she produces, such as wood, herbs, and the like.

I also said that the seeker of <term>Nature</term> <note>Nature is here described as an intelligent, creative force acting as a servant to the divine will.</note> ought to be truthful, simple, patient, and constant, applying their mind to at least one thing. Now we must begin to speak of the activity of nature: just as Nature exists within the will of God, and God created her—or rather, imposed her upon every <term>imagination</term> <note>original: "imaginationi." In this context, it refers to the formative power or the conceptual blueprint of the universe.</note>—so Nature made for herself a <term>seed</term> <note>original: "sperm." In alchemical theory, "sperma" or seed is the concentrated essence containing the specific "program" or "will" for a substance's development.</note>, that is, her will within the <term>Elements</term>. Nature is indeed one, and yet she brings forth diverse things; however, she performs nothing without a seed. Whatever the seed wills, Nature performs, for she is like the tool of a certain Craftsman. Therefore, the seed of each and every thing is more vital and useful to the <term>Craftsman</term> <note>original: "Artifex." This refers to the alchemist who works with natural materials to perfect them.</note> than Nature herself: for from Nature without a seed, you will accomplish only as much as—

In a similar way, from the center of the earth, the **seed** or **sperm**—projected by the **four elements** into the center—passes to various places, and the thing is formed according to the **Nature** of that place. If it reaches a place of earth and water that is pure, the resulting thing will be pure. By such inhuman and detestable mixing of men with beasts, various beasts would be produced similar to men...

In this alchemical context, it refers to a universal formative essence or "star-seed" rather than strictly biological fluid.</note></term> enters the center, that which ought to be born from it is born. But once it has come into its place and has conceived, it no longer changes its form. However, while the seed is still in the center, a tree can just as easily be produced from that seed as a metal, a herb as quickly as a stone; and one thing is more precious than another according to the purity of the place.

But how the **Elements** generate the seed must now be told by us, and it happens in this way: There are four Elements—two are heavy and two are light, two are dry and two are moist; but one is most dry and another most moist, and they are masculine and feminine, and so on. Each of these is most ready by itself to produce things similar to itself within its own sphere, and so the **Most High** <note>original: "Altissimus," referring to the Creator.</note> willed it. These four do not rest, but always act upon one another, and each by itself emits its own subtle substance, and they meet in the center.

In the center, however, is the <term>Archaeus<note>original: "Archæus." A term central to Paracelsian alchemy, the Archaeus is the "internal chemist" or vital force that separates and directs the elements to their proper forms.</note></term>, the servant of **Nature**, who by mixing those seeds casts them outward. How this is done, however, can be seen more fully in the Epilogue of these twelve **Treatises**.

### THIRD TREATISE.
# CONCERNING THE TRUE FIRST
## OF METALS
### *matter.*

---

The first matter of Metals is twofold, but one without the other does not create a metal: The first and principal is the <term>Moistness of the air</term> <note>original: "Humidum aëris"; this refers to a subtle, vaporous substance believed to be the life-fluid of the cosmos.</note> mixed with heat; this the Philosophers have named <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: "Mercurius"; in alchemy, this is the volatile, fluid, and feminine principle of all matter, not merely the common liquid metal.</note>, which is governed by the rays of the sun and moon in the <term>philosophical sea</term> <note>original: "mari philosophico"; a metaphor for the vessel or the state of matter where the elements dissolve and interact.</note>: The second is the dry heat of the earth, which they have called <term>sulfur</term> <note>original: "sulphur"; the active, masculine, and combustible principle that provides form and solidity.</note>: but because all true Philosophers have hidden this most especially, we shall explain it a little more clearly—especially the <term>weight</term> <note>original: "pondus"; this refers to the specific proportions or "dosage" of the ingredients, which was considered the ultimate secret of the Great Work.</note>, for if this is ignored, everything is destroyed: from this it happens that many produce <note>original: "abortus"; the author uses the term "abortions" to describe failed chemical reactions or experiments that do not reach maturity.</note> abortions out of a good thing: for there are those who take the whole body as the matter or seed, or sperm, while others take only a fragment; and all these deviate from the right path. For example, if someone were to take a man’s foot and a woman’s hand, and wished to create a human being from this mixture, it would not be possible.

<summary>This page defines the two essential components of metals as "Mercury" (moist air/heat) and "Sulfur" (dry earth heat), stressing that they must be combined in the correct proportions or "weight." The author warns that using whole substances or mismatched parts rather than the refined "seed" of the metals will result in failed alchemical experiments.</summary>

<keywords>First Matter, Mercury, Sulfur, Alchemical Weight, Philosophical Sea, Metallic Seed, Proportions</keywords>

...possible. For in every body there is a center, and a location or point of the seed or sperm, and it is always the 8,200th part <note>original: "8200. pars"; the author claims that the vital essence or "seed" of a substance is an incredibly tiny fraction—specifically 1/8200th—of its total mass.</note>, even in a single grain of wheat; and this cannot be otherwise. For the entire grain or body is not turned into seed; rather, there is only a certain necessary spark <note>original: "scintilla"; a vital, energetic spark of life or essence believed to reside in all matter.</note> within the body, which is protected by the rest of its body from all excessive heat and cold, and so forth. 

If you have ears and senses, pay attention here and you will be safe—not only from those who are ignorant of the seed's location and strive to reduce the entire grain into seed, but also from all those who engage in the vain dissolution of metals and wish to dissolve metals entirely so that they might later create a new metal from their mutual mixing. 

But if these people considered the process of Nature <note>original: "Naturae processum"; the alchemical principle that the chemist must mimic the slow, natural growth of minerals within the Earth.</note>, they would see that the matter behaves far differently. For no metal is so pure that it does not also have its impurities, though one may have more or fewer than another. But you, friend reader, shall first observe the "point of Nature," <note>original: "Naturae punctum"; the concentrated center of life or growth within a substance.</note> as stated above, and you will have enough; but

# ## CONCERNING MERCURY. ##

->TRAC-<- <note>Catchword for the following page, likely "TRACTATUS" (Treatise).</note>

Keep this caution for yourself: do not seek that "point" <note>The "point" refers to the essential spark or "seed" of the metal discussed on the previous page.</note> in the metals of the common people, for it is not in them. These metals, and especially <term>common gold</term> <note>original: "aurum vulgi"; this refers to ordinary metallic gold used in coins or jewelry, which alchemists considered "dead" because it had been melted and worked.</note>, are dead; but our metals are living and possess a spirit, and these are the ones you must use. Know indeed that the life of metals is fire while they are still in their ores, and their death is also fire—namely, the fire of the smelting furnace <note>original: "fusionis"; the process of melting or casting.</note>.

But the <term>first matter</term> <note>original: "prima materia"; the primordial substance from which all physical things are formed.</note> of metals is a certain moisture mixed with warm air; it takes the form of a "fatty water" <note>original: "aquæ pinguis"; a common alchemical term for a thick, oily, or vaporous substance that precedes physical metal.</note> that adheres to every thing, whether pure or impure. It is found more abundantly in one place than another because the earth in certain locations is more open and porous, possessing a greater attractive power.

Sometimes this matter emerges into the light on its own, clothed in a certain "garment," especially in places where it has nothing to adhere to. It is recognized in this way: although every thing is composed of the <term>three principles</term> <note>original: "tribus principijs"; the "Tria Prima" of Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury which alchemists believed formed all matter.</note>, in the matter of metals, it exists as a single thing without conjunction—save for its "garment" or shadow, namely <term>sulfur</term>, etc.

<summary>The author distinguishes between "dead" commercial metals and "living" alchemical metals, explaining that the "first matter" of metals is a vaporous, fatty moisture that exists within the earth before it is fixed into a physical form.</summary>

### FOURTH TREATISE.

# HOW METALS ARE GENERATED
## *in the bowels of the earth.*

Metals are produced in this manner: after the four Elements have projected their virtues <note>The "virtues" are the essential powers or qualities—hot, cold, moist, and dry—that define the elements.</note> into the center of the earth, the <term>Archaeus</term> <note>original: "Archæus"; in alchemical philosophy, this is the invisible "inner craftsman" or vital force that directs the formation of minerals and living things.</note>, through a process of distillation, sublimates them toward the surface of the earth by the heat of perpetual motion. For the earth is porous, and as the wind <note>original: "ventus"; here used to describe a gaseous or vaporous state that condenses as it moves.</note> distills through the pores of the earth, it is resolved into water, from which all things are born.

Therefore, let the sons of doctrine <note>A traditional term for students and initiates of the alchemical arts.</note> know that the seed of metals is no different from the seed of all other things: it is a moist vapor. Because of this, alchemists <note>original: "Artistæ"; literally "Artists," referring to those who practice the Hermetic Art.</note> seek in vain to reduce metals back into <term>first matter</term> <note>original: "materia prima"; the most basic, undifferentiated substance of the universe.</note>, which is nothing more than vapor. The Philosophers <note>The ancient authorities and masters of alchemy.</note> did not mean that specific "first matter," but rather <term>second matter</term> <note>original: "materia secunda"; the substance that has already begun to take on a specific form (like an ore or "fatty water") but is not yet a finished metal.</note>, just as <term>Bernard of Trevisan</term> <note>original: "Bernardus Treuisanus"; a famous 15th-century alchemist who emphasized that the "matter" of the work must be natural and not chemically "killed" by harsh acids.</note> very well argues—though perhaps not quite so clearly, since he speaks of the four Elements; nevertheless, he [said...]

<meta>catchword: said [di-]</meta>

<summary>This page describes the alchemical process of metal formation, explaining how a vital force called the Archaeus drives vapors from the earth's center to the surface. It clarifies that the "first matter" alchemists seek is actually a moist vapor and introduces the distinction between first and second matter.</summary>

<keywords>generation of metals, Archaeus, first matter, second matter, Bernard of Trevisan, distillation, subterranean vapor, four elements</keywords>

<meta>Continuity check: This page continues the discussion from page 23 regarding the "sperm" of metals and the transition from the first matter (vapor) to the second matter. It concludes the reference to Bernard Trevisan's cryptic style.</meta>

->CONCERNING MERCURY.<-

[[page number: 24]]

...to express it, and spoke only with his sons <note>original: "filijs"; referring to the "Sons of Wisdom" or alchemical initiates.</note>. I, however, so that I might more clearly uncover the theory, wished to warn everyone here to let be so many solutions, so many circulations, so many calcinations and repetitions; for that is sought in vain in a hard substance which is everywhere soft by its very nature. One should seek not the first, but only the second matter—such a matter, to be sure, that once it is conceived, it cannot be changed into another form. But if you ask how a metal is reduced into such a matter, I follow in this the intention of the Philosophers. This only I desire above all others: that the <term>sons of art</term> <note>original: "filij artis"; the practitioners and students of the alchemical "Great Work."</note> understand the meaning of the writers, not the literal syllables. Where Nature ends—namely, in the metallic, perfected bodies right before our eyes—there the art must begin. But to return to the proposal (since our intention here is not concerning the stone <note>The "Philosopher's Stone."</note> alone), let us now discuss the matter of metals. A little while ago, I said that all things are born from liquid air or vapor, which the Elements, by perpetual motion, distill into the bowels of the earth. After the <term>Archeus</term> <note>In alchemical philosophy, the Archeus is the "master craftsman" or vital spirit of nature that directs the growth and transformation of matter.</note> of Nature has received this,

For the more purified a place is, the more beautiful the metals it yields. It must be known that this vapor continually exits from the center toward the surface, purifying the places as it goes. From this it happens that today minerals <note>original: *minerae*; here meaning ore deposits or veins of metal</note> are found in places where a thousand years ago there were none. For in its progress, it always refines the crude and the impure, gradually drawing a little of it along with itself.

And this is the repetition and **circulation of Nature**: it is sublimated for so long, producing new things, until the place is perfectly purified; and the more it is purified, the more noble the things it produces.

But in winter, when the cold air constricts the earth, that <term>unctuous|oily or fatty; used to describe the "Mercury of the Philosophers" which was thought to be a thick, life-giving vapor</term> vapor also congeals. ->THE FIFTH TREATISE.<-
# CONCERNING THE GENERATION
## OF STONES OF EVERY
->*kind.*<-

The matter of a stone is the same as that of other things, and they are born in this way according to the purity of the locations. When the four <term>Elements|Earth, Air, Fire, and Water; the fundamental building blocks of the physical world</term> drip their vapor into the center of the earth, and the <term>Archaeus|the internal craftsman or vital force of Nature thought to govern the growth of minerals and living things</term> of Nature ejects and **sublimates** that same vapor, it passes through the spaces and pores of the earth. In doing so, it carries all the impurity of the earth with it up to the surface, where the air later congeals it. For what the pure air creates, the air also congeals.

The vapor, however, ascends to its destined places and is congealed in pure water into stones, grain by grain.

As for how colors are produced in gems, it should be understood that this happens by means of sulfur in this way: if the fatness of sulfur is congealed, then by that perpetual motion <note>Referring to the constant circulation of vapors within the earth's crust.</note>, the spirit of water, as it passes through, digests and purifies it by the power of salt, until it is colored with a "digested" red or white hue. This color—tending toward a more perfected state along with that spirit because it has been refined—is lifted up with the spirit through many repeated <term>distillations</term>. Afterward, the spirit has the power of entering things...

->CONCERNING MERCURY.<-

[[page-num: 32]]

...congeals in trees, and when these are dissolved by the **heat of the sun** (in which resides a **magnetic force** <note>The "magnetic force" of the sun refers to the solar power of attraction that draws moisture upward from the earth, acting as a celestial magnet for humidity.</note> that attracts all humidity), then the **heat of nature**, awakened through motion, drives a subtle water vapor toward the outer edges. This vapor opens the tree's pores and causes drops to distill, always separating the pure from the impure. 

Sometimes, however, the pure precedes the impure: the pure remains and congeals into flowers, while the impure passes into the leaves, and the coarse and thick matter into the bark. The tree's bark remains fixed, but the leaves fall in the cold or heat when the pores are obstructed. The flowers congeal into a color that matches the quality of the heat, and thus the tree brings forth fruit and seed. 

Take, for example, an apple: in it is the flesh <note>original: *sperma*. In this context, the author uses "sperma" to describe the protective, fleshy housing of a fruit, as distinct from the "semen" or actual seed.</note>, from which the tree does not grow. Rather, within that flesh is the **seed** or grain <term>intrinsically|existing as a fundamental, internal essence</term>, from which the tree is born even without the flesh. For multiplication occurs not in the flesh, but in the seed. Thus, we see with our own eyes how **Nature** creates seed from the **four elements**, so that we need not labor at this task in vain; for that which is already created requires no creator. Let this serve as an example...

It is necessary, therefore, either that the **four elements** create the seed of metals, or that they produce them without seed. If they are produced without seed, then they cannot become perfect, since...

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the nature of Mercury, moving from its philosophical definition to a practical warning against the errors of common chemists who mistake the body of a metal for its generative seed.</meta>

# CONCERNING MERCURY.

...will be made clear by the following example. It is manifest that men have seed, in which they are multiplied. A man’s body is <term>Mercury<note>Original: Mercurius. The author is using the term here to describe the physical "chassis" or substance of a body, distinct from its reproductive essence.</note></term>, but the seed is hidden within that body; and in proportion to the body, its quantity by weight is very small. Therefore, he who wishes to generate a human being must not take the Mercury (which is the body), but rather the seed, which is a "congealed vapor of water." In the same way, the process for the **regeneration of metals** is handled wrongly by common chemists. They dissolve metallic bodies—whether they be Mercury, or gold, or <term>Saturn<note>The alchemical name for lead.</note></term>, or <term>the Moon<note>Original: Lunam. The alchemical name for silver.</note></term>—and they corrode them with <term>aqua fortis<note>Literally "strong water"; a historical term for nitric acid.</note></term> and other heterogeneous substances not required for the true Art. Afterwards, they join these things together, not realizing that a human being cannot be generated from a dissected human body, because the corruption of the body and the destruction of the seed have already occurred through that method. Every single thing is multiplied in male and female, as I mentioned in the treatise concerning the "double matter." The mere division of the sexes creates or produces nothing; rather, it is their proper union that brings forth a new form. Therefore, seeds or <term>sperma<note>Original: spermata. Refers to the vital, reproductive essence or "starting material" of a substance.</note></term> are to be taken, not the solid bodies themselves.

->CONCERNING MERCURY.<-

Take <note>Original: ℞, the traditional symbol for 'Recipe' or 'Take'.</note> therefore the living male and the living female; join these together so that they may conceive <note>Original: "imaginentur," here used in the sense of 'forming an image' or 'bringing into being' through internal conception.</note> a seed between them, for the procreation of fruit according to their Nature. For let no mortal believe that he is able to make the <term>first matter</term> <note>First matter (materia prima) is the primordial, unformed substance of the universe. Alchemists argued that only God can create this from nothing, while the chemist works with what already exists.</note>. The first matter of man is the earth, and no man is able to create a human from it; God alone knows this. 

But from <term>second matter</term> <note>Second matter (materia secunda) refers to substance that has already been given a specific potential by Nature, such as a seed or a particular mineral, which the alchemist can then refine.</note>, which has already been created, if it is placed in the proper location, that thing whose form is in the seed will be easily generated through the operation of Nature. The craftsman <note>The alchemist or 'Artifex'.</note> here does nothing, except that he knows how to separate the <term>subtle</term> <note>The volatile, pure, or spiritual essence of a substance.</note> from the <term>dense</term> <note>The gross, solid, or impure physical remains.</note>, and to place it in the proper vessel. 

For this must be considered: as a thing is begun, so it is finished. From one come two; from two, one; and no further. God is One, and from this one God the Son was begotten: One gave two, and the two gave one—the Holy Spirit proceeding from both. Thus was the world made, and thus shall be its end. Consider these prior four points most exactly: you have in them the Father, the Father and the Son, and finally the Holy Spirit <note>Original: "SS." (Spiritum Sanctum)</note>. You have the four Elements; you have the four luminaries, two Celestial, two

->CONCERNING MERCURY.<-

The central points <note>original: "Centralia", referring to the essential truths or perhaps the center of a diagram mentioned in the text.</note> are nothing more than what is, was, and shall be, as is evident in this figure. If I were required to record all the mysteries to be drawn from this, a massive volume would be produced. I return to my purpose, and I tell you the truth, my son: one is not created from one, because this is the property of God alone; let it suffice that you are able to create from two a single thing that is useful to you. Know, therefore, that the multiplicative seed <note>original: "sperma multiplicativum"</note> is the fruitful matter, and not the <term>first matter</term> <note>The primordial, unformed substance of the universe which alchemists believed only God could create or perceive.</note>. For the first matter of things is not seen; it is hidden in Nature or in the Elements, but the <term>second matter</term> <note>Substance that has already been given a specific potential by Nature, such as a seed or a particular mineral, which the alchemist can then refine.</note> occasionally appears to the "sons of doctrine" <note>A traditional term for initiated students or practitioners of alchemy.</note>.

It is necessary, therefore, for the pores of bodies to be opened so that the *sperm* <term>sperma: the visible, physical fluid that acts as a carrier for the generative essence</term> may be emitted. In the center of this sperm lies the *seed* <term>semen: the invisible, spiritual "spark" or formative principle of life</term>, which is air. As long as the seed remains in the body, the body lives; when it is entirely consumed, the body dies. Moreover, all bodies are weakened after the emission of the seed. Experience itself testifies that men who indulge too much in Venus <note>original: "veneri"; a traditional reference to sexual activity or physical lust</note> become weaker, just as trees that bear too much fruit later become sterile. 

The seed, therefore, as has been repeated many times, is an invisible thing, but the sperm is visible, and it is almost a living soul; it is not found in dead things. It is drawn out in two ways: gently and with violence. But because the discussion here concerns its power, I say that nothing is born without seed, and all things are made by the power of the seed. Let the *sons of the art* <term>artis filij: a traditional term for true alchemical practitioners who understand the secrets of nature</term> know that it is in vain to seek the seed in cut-down trees, for it is only found in those that are green and living.

### THE EIGHTH TREATISE.
# ON ART, AND
### HOW THROUGH ART
### *nature operates in the Seed.*

Every seed by itself is of no importance if it is not placed, either by Art <term>Ars: the practice of alchemy, where the human operator assists or accelerates natural processes</term> or by Nature, into its proper matrix. And although the seed itself may be more noble than any creature, nevertheless its matrix is its life, which causes the sperm <term>sperma: the physical carrier or grain</term> or the grain to putrefy <note>In alchemy, putrefaction is the necessary first stage of transformation, where a substance breaks down so its inner life-force can be released.</note> and causes the coagulation of the pure point <note>original: "puri puncti"; the concentrated essence or "spark" of life within the matter</note>. Furthermore, by the heat of its own body, the matrix nourishes the seed and makes it grow. This occurs in all three of the aforementioned kingdoms of nature <note>The Mineral, Vegetable, and Animal kingdoms.</note>, and it happens naturally over months, years, and seasons.

However, that Art is wise which can shorten the process in the mineral and vegetable kingdoms, though not in the animal kingdom. In the mineral kingdom, Art perfects only that which nature cannot, because of the "crude air" <note>original: "aërem crudum"; the raw, unbalanced atmospheric conditions on the Earth's surface compared to the protected environment deep underground</note> which, by its violence, has filled the pores of every body—not in the bowels of the earth, but on the surface of the earth, as I have said before in the preceding chapters. But so that it may be more easily underst[ood]—

...stood this, I wished to add further that the Elements, as if in competition with one another, cast their seed into the center of the earth, just as if into the kidneys <note>original: "renes"; in early modern physiology and alchemy, the kidneys were often associated with the production and filtering of generative fluids.</note>. These matrices are indeed innumerable; as many places as there are, so many matrices exist—one purer than another, and so on almost to infinity. Know therefore, that a pure matrix will produce a pure offspring in its own likeness. For example, among animals, there are matrices for humans, cows, horses, dogs, and so on; likewise, in the mineral and vegetable kingdoms, there are metals, stones, and salts. For salts must be considered within these two kingdoms, along with their locations, according to their greater or lesser degree of purity.

# ->CONCERNING MERCURY.<-

I approach [this task]. The **Philosopher’s Stone** <note>original: *Lapis Philosophorum*</note> or **tincture** <note>A 'tincture' in alchemy refers to a potent substance—often the Stone itself—capable of transmuting base metals by "dyeing" or infusing them with its perfected quality.</note> is nothing other than **Gold** <note>original: *Aurum*</note> digested to the highest degree; for common gold is like a plant without seed, but when a plant matures, it produces seed. In the same way, when gold matures, it yields its seed or tincture. 

But someone may ask: why does gold or any other metal not produce seed? The reason provided is this: because it cannot reach maturity due to the **harshness of the air** <note>original: *aëris cruditatem*. Alchemists believed that the external environment and local temperature (the 'air') could either nourish or stifle the growth of metals within the earth's 'womb'.</note>; it lacks sufficient heat. It happens in certain places that pure gold is found, which nature intended to perfect, but was hindered by this harsh air. 

For example, we see that **orange trees** <note>original: *Pomorum Arantiorum*</note> grow in **Poland** indeed just like other trees; but in **Italy** and elsewhere, where the soil is their native home, they bear and yield fruit because they have sufficient heat. In these colder places, however, they do not at all; for where they ought to mature, they stop because they are overwhelmed by the cold, and thus their fruits are never obtained from **Nature** in these places—though sometimes Nature may be assisted gently and with ingenuity <note>original: *ingenio*. This refers to the 'Art' of alchemy, where human skill and technology (like controlled furnace heat) are used to complete what nature could not finish due to environmental limitations.</note>,

Exactly the same happens in metals. **Gold** <note>original: *Aurum*</note> can yield fruit and seed, in which it multiplies itself through the industry of a wise craftsman <note>original: *sagacis artificis*</note> who knows how to encourage Nature; but if he wishes to attempt this without Nature, he will go astray. For not only in this Art, but in every matter, we are able to do nothing but assist **Nature**, and this by no other means than by **fire** or **heat**. 

Yet, since this cannot happen—seeing as **spirits** <note>In alchemical theory, 'spirits' are the volatile, life-giving components of a substance that are trapped inside solid, 'congealed' matter.</note> do not appear in a congealed metallic body—it is necessary that the body first be dissolved and its pores opened, so that Nature may be able to work. Regarding what kind of solution that ought to be, I would here wish to warn the reader that there is a twofold solution. Although there are many other solutions, they are useless; only one is true and natural, while the second is **violent** <note>original: *violenta*. A 'violent' solution usually refers to the use of corrosive acids that destroy the substance's 'vitality' rather than gently opening it.</note>, under which all others are included. 

The natural solution is of such a kind that the pores of the body are opened in **our water** <note>original: *aqua nostra*. This refers to the 'Philosophic Mercury,' a specialized solvent believed to dissolve metals without destroying their essential 'seed.'</note>, whereby the digested seed may be emitted and placed into its **matrix** <note>The 'matrix' is the alchemical 'womb' or vessel where the seed of the metal is nurtured into the Philosopher's Stone.</note>. Our water, however, is a **celestial water**, which does not wet the hands; it is not the water of the common people, but is almost like **rainwater**:

# CONCERNING MERCURY.

...grains, 1 grain of <term>our gold</term> <note>original: "auri nostri"; in alchemy, this is the purified masculine principle, not common metallic gold.</note> and not the common variety. 11 grains of <term>our Moon</term> <note>original: "Lunae nostrae"; refers to the purified silver or feminine principle.</note> and not the common variety: but be warned of this, that you do not take the gold and silver of the common people, for these are dead. Take ours, which are living; afterwards, place them into <term>our fire</term> <note>original: "ignem nostrum"; the specialized, controlled heat of the alchemist’s furnace.</note> and from that will be made a <term>dry liquor</term> <note>original: "liquor siccus"; a philosophical substance that behaves like a fluid but does not wet the hands.</note>. First, the earth will be resolved into water, which is called the <term>Mercury of the Philosophers</term> <note>original: "Mercurius Philosophorum"; the essential universal solvent used to break down the metals.</note>, and that water will resolve those bodies of the <term>Sun and Moon</term> <note>original: "Solis & Lunae"; alchemical code for gold and silver.</note> and will consume them, so that there remains nothing but a tenth part with one part, and this will be the <term>metallic radical moisture</term> <note>original: "humidum radicale metallicum"; the essential, oily life-force within the metal that survives the dissolution.</note>.

Afterward, **take** <note>original: "℞" (recipe/recipe symbol)</note> the water of <term>salt nitre</term> <note>original: "salis nitri"; while literally saltpeter, in this context it refers to a philosophical salt extracted from "our earth."</note> from our earth, in which there is a stream and a living wave, if you dig a pit to the depth of a knee; take therefore the water from it, but take only the clear water. To this, add that radical moisture and place it in the <term>fire of putrefaction</term> <note>original: "igni putrefactionis"; a gentle heat that causes the matter to blacken and "die" before being reborn.</note> and generation—not such a fire as you used in the first operation. Govern all things with great skill, until colors appear like the <term>Peacock's Tail</term> <note>original: "caudæ Pauonis"; a celebrated stage in alchemy where the matter in the vessel displays a shimmering variety of colors.</note>. Rule the matter by digesting it, and do not let it weary you, until the colors cease and one **green color** <note>original: "color viridis"; often called the "Green Lion," this stage signifies the beginning of new life in the matter.</note> appears throughout. So it goes with the other stages, and when you see brown ashes in the bottom...

For know that you can create nothing—which is a power belonging to God alone—but you can make visible those hidden things lying in the shadow and remove the shadow from them; this is granted to the wise Philosopher <note>original: "Philosopho intelligenti." I pray you, consider the simple water of a cloud: Who would ever believe that it contains within itself everything the world holds—hard stones, salts, air, earth, and fire—when, nevertheless, it appears as simple water in itself? What shall I say of the earth, which contains within itself water, fire, salts, and air, yet appears as bare earth in itself? O wonderful nature, which knows how to produce marvelous fruits from water in the earth...

For example, the eyes of the common people see the Sun as being hot, but the eyes of the Philosophers <note>In this context, "Philosophers" refers to alchemists and natural philosophers who look beyond surface appearances to the hidden mechanics of the universe.</note>, on the contrary, see the Sun as being rather cold, but its movements as hot; for its actions and effects are understood through the distance of places. For just as the Sun is the center among the spheres of the Planets, and from this center of heaven it scatters heat downward by its motion, so in the center of the earth is the **Sun of the Earth** <note>Also known as the "Central Fire," this refers to an internal heat source within the globe that alchemists believed was responsible for "ripening" metals and minerals.</note>, which by its perpetual motion drives heat or rays upward to the surface of the earth.

Likewise, the celestial Sun and its heat are tempered by the air, which from day to day flies around the world; and if this were not so, by such heat...

### ON MERCURY.

...all things would be consumed, and nothing would be born. Just as that invisible fire <note>In alchemical thought, "invisible fire" refers to the vital, creative spirit or "Archeus" that drives natural processes from within.</note>, acting as a central heat, would consume all things if water did not intervene, so too would the heat of the Sun destroy all things without the mediating presence of the air. I shall explain briefly how these Elements <note>Earth, Air, Fire, and Water—the four building blocks of the universe in pre-modern science.</note> act upon one another.

In the center of the earth is a **central Sun** <note>original: "Sol centralis"; the internal source of the earth’s warmth, distinct from the Sun in the sky.</note>, which by its own motion, or the motion of its firmament, provides a great heat that extends all the way to the earth's surface. That heat generates air in this manner: the womb <note>original: "matrix"; used here to mean the source or environment where a substance is generated.</note> of air is water, which gives birth to offspring of its own nature, yet different and much more subtle. For where the entry of water is denied, air enters; later, when that perpetual central heat acts, it causes the water to distill and grow warm, and thus by the power of heat, that water is turned into air.

In this way, it breaks out toward the surface of the earth because it does not allow itself to be trapped. Once it has cooled there, it is resolved back into water. Meanwhile, in other places, it also happens that not only air but even water escapes, just as it appears when black clouds are violently raised into the air. Let this be an example for you...

...and this happens without ceasing, by **perpetual motion**, because fire or heat is not excited in any other way except by motion. This is easily seen in the case of a **blacksmith** <note>original: "fabrum ferrarium"</note> filing down iron with a file; the iron grows hot through that more vehement motion, just as if it had been made glowing hot by a fire. Motion, therefore, causes heat; heat moves the water, and the motion of water causes the air, which is the life of all living things.

Therefore, things grow in this manner, as I have said above—namely, from water. For from its more subtle vapor, subtle and light things arise; from its oil, however, come heavy things of greater value; and from its salt come things far more beautiful than the former.

# EPILOGUE OR
## CONCLUSION OF THESE
### *Twelve Treatises.*

I have written, friend reader, the preceding twelve Treatises for the sake of the sons of art <note>A traditional term for alchemists and practitioners of the hermetic sciences who considered themselves spiritual "descendants" of the masters.</note>, so that before they set their hands to the work, they might have knowledge of nature’s operation—namely, how she produces things by her working—lest they should enter the gate without keys and draw water with a sieve <note>A metaphor for a futile or impossible task.</note>. For he labors in vain who stretches out his hands to the tasks without a knowledge of nature: in this holy and most true science, he who is not lit by the sun dwells in nocturnal darkness, in a thick shadow

is he, to whom the moon does not appear at night. Nature possesses its own light <note>Latin: *lumen naturae*. A central concept in Renaissance alchemy and philosophy (notably used by Paracelsus) referring to an innate wisdom or spiritual light within the natural world that allows one to understand the "signatures" of things.</note>, which is not visible to our eyes; the shadow of nature is what our eyes perceive as a solid body. However, if the light of nature shines upon anyone, the mist is immediately lifted from their eyes, and they can perceive without hindrance the point of our magnet <note>Latin: *magnetis*. In alchemy, the "magnet" often refers to a substance or spiritual force capable of attracting the "spirit of the world" (spiritus mundi) or the vital influence of the stars.</note> that corresponds to both centers of radiation—namely, those of the sun and the earth. For the light of nature penetrates that far and lays open the internal secrets. 

Let this be an example for you of this matter: if a twelve-year-old boy and a girl of the same age are dressed in identical clothing and placed next to each other, no one will know who is male and who is female, for our eyes cannot penetrate through the garments. Thus, our sight deceives us and accepts the false for the true. But when the clothes are removed and they are stripped bare, so that they appear as nature formed them, each is easily distinguished by their sex. In exactly the same way, our intellect creates a shadow for the shadow of nature: for the naked body in a human being is but the shadow of nature's seed <note>Latin: *seminis naturae*. This refers to the essential spiritual spark or quintessence that precedes the formation of a physical body.</note>. Just as the human body is covered by clothing, so also

# TO THE SONS OF TRUTH

## PREFACE TO THE PHILOSOPHICAL ENIGMA.

Now for you, sons of science <note>original: "filij fcientiæ" (sons of science); a common term of endearment for students of alchemy and hermetic philosophy</note>, I have uncovered everything from the bubbling source <note>original: "scaturigine" (a spring or headwater)</note> of the universal fountain, so that nothing more remains to be said. For in my previous treatises, I have sufficiently explained nature through examples: I have revealed both the theory <term>Theorica: the intellectual or spiritual principles of alchemy</term> and the practice <term>Praxis: the physical laboratory operations</term>, as much as was divinely permitted <note>original: "quantum fas fuit" (as much as was lawful or right according to divine will)</note>. 

But lest anyone should complain of brevity <term>Laconismo: a style of speaking or writing that is very concise, named after the Laconians or Spartans</term>, as if I had omitted something, I will further describe the whole art to you in the form of an enigma <note>The "Philosophical Enigma" is a traditional way for alchemists to hide their secrets in plain sight using allegories or riddles that only the "worthy" would understand.</note>, so that you may see how far I have progressed under the guidance of God. There are infinite books treating this art, yet in hardly any will you find the truth revealed to you to such an extent; I wished to provide this for the reason that since...

->CONCERNING MERCURY.<-

I have conversed with many people who believed they understood the writings of the Philosophers well, but I discovered that they interpreted those same writings far more intricately than nature—which is simple—required. In fact, all my truthful sayings seemed too common and unbelievable to those who were always pondering high-minded things <note>original: "alta sapientibus" (thinking high things); a reference to those who over-intellectualize and miss the simplicity of natural processes.</note>. 

Sometimes it happened that I would explain the art word for word to certain individuals, yet they were not at all able to grasp it, refusing to believe that water exists in our sea <note>The "sea" of the alchemists is a common metaphor for the primary substance or "mercury" used in their operations; the "water" is the life-giving essence within it.</note>, even though they wanted to be regarded as Philosophers. 

Since, therefore, they could not understand my words even when spoken face-to-face, I do not fear (as other Philosophers have feared) that anyone might grasp this too easily; it is a gift, I say, of God. It is indeed true that if the study of chemistry <term>Chymico: in this period, the term referred to the practical and philosophical study of matter and its transformations, often synonymous with alchemy</term> required the sharpest mental acuity, and if the matter were such that it could be seen by the eyes of the common people, I have seen their intellects to be well enough suited for investigating such things. 

But I tell you: be simple, and not over-clever, until you have discovered the secret <term>arcanum: a profound mystery or a hidden natural force; in alchemy, it often refers to the ultimate transformative substance</term>. Once you possess it, wisdom <note>original: "prudentia"; here meaning the practical wisdom or skill to apply the secret knowledge</note> will necessarily follow; then you will not find yourself lacking in infinite books

<summary>The author explains that many would-be philosophers fail to understand the Great Work because they over-complicate nature, which is inherently simple. He emphasizes that alchemy is a gift from God that requires a simple mind rather than intellectual vanity.</summary>

<keywords>simplicity of nature, alchemical sea, divine gift, arcanum, intellectual pride, Michael Sendivogius, hermetic philosophy</keywords>

you will not reveal yourself to the

He took the fruit of the Solar Tree <note>original: *Arboris Solaris*; in alchemy, the Solar Tree typically represents Gold or the "active" masculine principle.</note> and placed it in the vessel, and I saw the fruit of the Tree be consumed and dissolved like ice in warm water. I asked of him, "Lord, I see a wondrous thing: this Water is produced almost from nothing; I see the fruits of the Tree being consumed within it by such a gentle heat. To what purpose are these things?"

And he graciously replied to me: "My son, it is true that this thing is wondrous, but do not marvel, for it must be so. For this Water is the Water of Life <note>original: *Aqua vitæ*; here referring to a potent alchemical solvent, likely a purified form of Mercury, rather than brandy or spirits.</note>, having the power to so improve the fruit of this tree that afterwards—not by planting or grafting, but by its scent alone—it makes the other six trees <note>The "six trees" represent the other six metals of antiquity: Silver, Copper, Iron, Tin, Lead, and Mercury (if Gold is the Solar Tree).</note> uniform with itself.

Furthermore, this Water is like a female to this fruit; in no other thing can the fruit of this tree rot <note>original: *putrefieri*; in alchemy, "putrefaction" is a necessary stage of decay that allows for spiritual rebirth and purification.</note>, except in this Water. And although the fruit is wondrous in itself and a precious thing, nevertheless, if it rots in this Water, it gives birth from this putrefaction to a Salamander <note>A mythical lizard believed to live in and endure fire; here it symbolizes the "Philosopher's Stone" or the final stage of the work that is perfected by heat.</note> persisting in the fire. Its blood is more precious than any treasure, having the power to make the six trees you see here fertile, and to bring forth their fruit sweeter than honey."

But I asked, "Lord, how—"

How can this be? "I told you," he said, "that the fruits of the Solar Tree <note>original: *Arboris Solaris*; symbols of Gold or the perfected masculine principle.</note> are living and sweet; but while now barely one person is satisfied by it, once it is cooked in this water, a thousand can afterwards be satisfied by it." <note>This refers to the "multiplication" of the Philosopher's Stone, where its potency increases so it can transform vast quantities of base metal.</note>

I asked further, "Lord, is it cooked with a strong fire, and for how long?"

But he replied, "This Water has an internal fire <note>original: *intrinsecum ignem*; a "secret fire" or inherent energy within the chemical substance that allows it to transform from within.</note>, and if it is aided by continuous heat, it consumes three parts of its own body along with the body of the fruit. Nothing will remain except a most tiny part, which can hardly be imagined, yet it exists in the highest virtue <term>virtue: in alchemy, this refers to the concentrated power or medicinal efficacy of a substance</term>. It is cooked by the wise skill of the Master, first for seven months, and afterwards for ten. But meanwhile, various things appear, always around the fiftieth day, more or less."

I asked yet again, "Lord, can this fruit not also be cooked in other waters, or is something added to it?"

He responded, "There is no other Water but this one which is useful in this region or island. No other Water can penetrate the pores of this fruit <note>original: *pomi*; literally 'apple', a common alchemical metaphor for the material of the Work.</note> except this; and know that the Solar Tree itself also arose from this Water, which was extracted from the rays of the Sun or Moon by the power of a magnet <note>original: *vi magnetis*; this describes "celestial agriculture," the belief that the "spirit of the world" could be drawn down from the stars and planets into a physical medium.</note>. Therefore, between them a great...

In alchemy, "Steel" often refers to a specific mineral or a "magnet" capable of attracting the "astral" influence</note>, which is found in the belly of the Ram <note>original: *Arietis*. This paradoxical phrase is a classic alchemical description of "Philosophical Mercury," which flows like a liquid but does not behave like common water.</note>."

"Every creature uses it," he said, "but invisibly."

"Does anything grow in it?" I inquired.

And he replied: "From it all things in the world are made, and in it they live; yet in it there is properly nothing, but it is a substance that mixes itself with every thing."

I asked: "Is it useful without this fruit of the Tree?"

To this he said: "In this work, not at all, for it is only improved in this fruit of the **Solar Tree** <note>The Solar Tree represents the finalized alchemical gold or the "Stone" in its perfected state.</note> alone."

I began to plead: "Lord, I beg you, name it for me with such a clear name that I can no longer have any doubt."

But he, raising his voice, cried out so loudly that he woke me from my sleep; therefore I could no longer ask, nor did he wish to answer me further, and I can tell you no more.

And he replied: "It is true, son, all generation occurs through a male <note>original: "mare." This is likely a typographical error in the original Latin text or the transcription for "mas" (male), given the contrast with "female" (foemina) and the discussion of biological reproduction.</note> and a female; but because of the division of the three kingdoms of Nature, a four-footed animal is born in one way, and a worm in another way. For although worms have eyes, sight, hearing, and the rest of the senses, they are nonetheless produced through putrefaction <term>putrefactio: the alchemical stage of "blackness" where matter decays; alchemists believed this "death" was the necessary first step to spiritual rebirth</term>, and their place, or the earth in which they rot, is the female. So in the Philosophical Work <term>Opus Philosophicum: the "Great Work" of creating the Philosopher's Stone</term>, the mother of this thing is that Water of yours, so often repeated; and whatever is born from it is born in the manner of worms, through putrefaction. Therefore, the Philosophers created the Phoenix and the Salamander <note>In alchemy, the Phoenix represents the "Red Work" or resurrection, while the Salamander represents the "Fixed" substance that can endure the fire without being consumed.</note>. For if it were made from the conception of two [distinct] bodies, it would be a thing subject to death; but because it revivifies itself, once the former body is destroyed, another, incorruptible one shines forth. For indeed, the death of things is nothing other than the separation of one part from another. And in this Phoenix, it happens in such a way that life separates itself from the corruptible body by its own power."

# CONCERNING MERCURY.

Furthermore, I asked: "My Lord, are there different substances involved, or a complex composition of things in this work?" And he replied: "It is a single substance, with which nothing else is mixed, except for the Philosophical Water <term>Philosophical Water (Latin: *Aqua Philosophica*): The universal solvent or mercurial agent used to dissolve the 'body' of a metal to begin its transformation.</term> that has been revealed to you several times in your sleep. This water must be in a tenfold proportion to one part of the body <note>In alchemy, the 'body' usually refers to the solid metallic base, such as gold or lead, which is to be dissolved or 'perfected'.</note>. And believe firmly and without doubt, my son, that those things revealed to you by me and by Saturn <note>Saturn is the alchemical personification of lead and the process of 'nigredo' or blackening/decay.</note> through dreams on this island—as is the custom of this region—are in no way mere dreams, but the purest truth. Experience, the only mistress of all things <note>A reference to the Latin proverb *experientia est magistra rerum*, emphasizing that practical work is the only way to prove alchemical theory.</note>, will be able to reveal this to you with the assistance of G O D." As I sought to ask further, he departed after bidding farewell without an answer; I was then awakened from sleep and restored to the longed-for region of Europe. And so, dear Reader, let what has been said by me so far suffice for you. Farewell.

# A DIALOGUE
## BETWEEN MERCURY,
## THE ALCHEMIST, AND
### NATURE.

At that time <note>original: *In illo tempore*. This phrasing mimics the beginning of liturgical readings or parables.</note>, the Alchemists gathered together and held a council to determine how they should create and prepare the **Philosopher’s Stone** <term>Philosopher’s Stone (Latin: *Lapis Philosophorum*): The legendary substance sought by alchemists for its ability to transmute base metals into gold and provide a universal medicine.</term>. They decided that everyone should take turns sharing their own opinion. This gathering took place under the open sky <note>original: *sub dio*</note> in a certain meadow on a clear day. 

Many agreed that Mercury <note>The liquid metal quicksilver, named after the messenger god for its fluidity and volatility.</note> was the first matter <term>First Matter (Latin: *materia prima*): The primordial, unformed substance that alchemists believed was the essential starting point for creating the Stone.</term> of the Stone; others argued for Sulphur, and others for different substances. But the prevailing opinion among them concerned Mercury, especially based on the writings of the ancient Philosophers, since they hold it to be the true first matter, and even the first matter of the Metals. For the Philosophers cry out: "**Our Mercury**" <note>original: *Noster Mercurius*. Alchemists often used this phrase to distinguish their secret "Philosophical Mercury" from the common metal found in a shop.</note> and so forth. And while they were thus contending among themselves through various labors—for each of them was waiting with desire...

<summary>This page introduces a group of alchemists meeting in a meadow to debate the identity of the "First Matter" needed to create the Philosopher's Stone. They conclude that "Our Mercury" is the most likely candidate based on the authority of ancient texts.</summary>
<keywords>Mercury, Alchemist, Nature, Philosopher's Stone, First Matter, Sulphur, Alchemical Council</keywords>

--- Page 90 ---
<meta>This page continues the narrative from page 85, where an assembly of alchemists was discussing the nature of "Our Mercury" as the primary matter of the Philosopher's Stone. The previous page ended with the catchword "Mercu-".</meta>

<page-num>86</page-num>
-># ON MERCURY.<-

-ry and joy <note>original: "Mercurio & lætitia"</note>), meanwhile a great storm arose with squalls, clouds, and an unheard-of wind, which dispersed that gathering into various provinces, each one separately and without a conclusion. Nevertheless, each of them fixed in his mind what the end of that disputation ought to be. Therefore, each began his labors as before, one in this thing, another in that, seeking the **PHILOSOPHER'S STONE**, and this continues until now without ceasing. 

But one of them, mindful of the disputation—that the Philosopher's Stone <term>Lapis Philosophorum|The ultimate goal of alchemy, believed to transform base metals into gold and grant long life.</term> must necessarily be sought in Mercury—said to himself: "Even if no conclusion was reached, nevertheless I will work with Mercury, and I myself will bring it to a conclusion by making the blessed stone"; for he was a man who was always accustomed to speak to himself, as Alchemists usually do. <note>The "solitary alchemist" talking to himself is a common trope in early modern literature, reflecting the isolated and secretive nature of the "Great Work."</note> 

Therefore, he began to read the Books of the Philosophers, and he happened upon the Book of Alanus, <note>Likely referring to Alanus de Insulis (Alan of Lille), a 12th-century scholar to whom several alchemical treatises were attributed.</note> which treats of Mercury, and thus that Alchemist became a Philosopher, but without a conclusion. And having taken Mercury, he began to work; he placed it in a glass vessel <note>original: "vitrum"</note> and applied fire.
